> Note on the prior `docs/reviews/milestone-2-review.md` finding C1 ("`userId = user.id` is compile-blocking"): I verified this is **not** a real defect. A clean recompile (`gradlew --no-build-cache clean compileKotlin`) succeeds — `LoginService.class` was regenerated at build time. The reason is Kotlin's flow-sensitive smart-casting: `user.id!!` on `LoginService.kt:35` asserts `user.id` non-null, and because `user` is a local `val` and `User.id` is a final `val` property, the compiler carries that non-null fact to `LoginService.kt:40`, so `userId = user.id` type-checks. The nullable `id: UUID?` pattern is still worth cleaning up (see Low), but it is not compile-blocking.

## High

### H1 — Login timing oracle discloses whether an email is registered
**File:** `apps/api/src/main/kotlin/com/productivityos/user/LoginService.kt:28-33`
When the email is unknown, `passwordEncoder.matches(...)` is never called, so the response returns in milliseconds; when the email exists but the password is wrong, Argon2id (memory-hard, tens–hundreds of ms) runs. The response bodies are identical, but the timing difference leaks registration status. This violates the User Management spec ("Failed logins receive a generic error that does not reveal whether the email is registered"), ADR-004 Security Considerations, and Plan 001 Step 6 ("wrong password and unknown email return identical generic 401").
**Fix:** run a dummy `passwordEncoder.matches(request.password, <constant precomputed Argon2id hash>)` when the user is absent, then throw the same `AuthenticationException`.

### H2 — Invalid timezone at registration surfaces as HTTP 500, not a structured 400
**Files:** `RegistrationService.kt:20-23` (`ZoneId.of(zone)` throws `ZoneRulesException`/`DateTimeException` for unknown IDs); `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` (only `AuthenticationException` and `IllegalArgumentException` are handled).
An unrecognized IANA identifier propagates to Spring's default handler → 500. AC-013 requires an invalid identifier to be **rejected** (4xx), and ADR-005 requires a structured error body.
**Fix:** add `@ExceptionHandler(DateTimeException::class)` → `400 {code:"INVALID_TIMEZONE"}` (or validate at the boundary in `RegisterRequest`).

### H3 — Bean-validation failures are not mapped to the ADR-005 structured error model
**Files:** `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` (no `MethodArgumentNotValidException`/`ConstraintViolationException` handler); `AuthController.kt:20,33` use `@Valid`.
A blank email or a <12-char password throws `MethodArgumentNotValidException`, which has no handler and falls through to Spring Boot's default `ProblemDetail` — a 400 in status, but not the project's structured contract (no stable `code`, no per-field `details`). Plan 001 Step 5 explicitly requires "invalid email and weak password → 400 with structured error (ADR-005)."
**Fix:** add `@ExceptionHandler(MethodArgumentNotValidException::class)` → `400 {code:"VALIDATION_ERROR", message, details:[{field,message}]}`.

### H4 — Duplicate-email under concurrency returns HTTP 500 (DB constraint exception unmapped)
**Files:** `RegistrationService.kt:16-18` (check-then-act `existsByEmail` then `save`); `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` (no `DataIntegrityViolationException` handler); `V2__users.sql:13` (unique index is the real backstop).
This is a TOCTOU race: two concurrent registrations with the same email can both pass `existsByEmail`, then the second `save` fails the unique index → `DataIntegrityViolationException` → 500, instead of the intended `409 EMAIL_TAKEN` (AC-002).
**Fix:** add `@ExceptionHandler(DataIntegrityViolationException::class)` → `409 {code:"EMAIL_TAKEN"}`, treating the DB constraint as authoritative (the pre-check can remain as a fast-path).

### H5 — No injectable `Clock`; all time access uses `Instant.now()` directly (ADR-006 violation)
**Files:** `TokenService.kt:25,41`; `User.kt:30`; `RefreshToken.kt:29,32`. No `Clock` bean exists anywhere in the source (verified by search).
ADR-006 is explicit: "All time access goes through an injectable `java.time.Clock`." Token `issuedAt`/`expiration`, `refreshTokenExpiry()`, `createdAt`, and `expiresAt` all call `Instant.now()` directly. This makes token-expiry and timestamp behavior non-deterministic in tests and will block Step 8 ("expiry enforced") and Step 18 ("injectable Clock fixed per test").
**Fix:** add `@Bean fun clock(): Clock = Clock.systemUTC()` and inject `Clock` into `TokenService` and entity-creation paths; establish the pattern now so later modules inherit it.

### H6 — No behavioral tests exist for the entire milestone; in-scope acceptance criteria are unverified
**Files:** `apps/api/src/test/kotlin/com/productivityos/` — only `ApplicationContextTest` (2 tests: context loads, health) and `ArchitectureTest` (1 test: cycles). Verified: 2 test files, 3 test methods, all smoke/structural.
Plan 001 Steps 3–6 each list required tests that are entirely absent: Step 3 (repository round-trip, email uniqueness), Step 4 (hash/verify round-trip), Step 5 (successful registration, duplicate email rejected, invalid email/weak password → 400), Step 6 (valid login issues both tokens, wrong password and unknown email return identical 401). AGENTS.md mandates "Every behavioral requirement should have verification." None of the in-scope User Management ACs (AC-001–AC-013) are verified by automated tests. The build and existing smoke tests do pass (verified), so this is a coverage gap, not an environment problem.
**Fix:** add the Step 3–6 behavioral tests against Testcontainers PostgreSQL (already wired via `@ServiceConnection`), including an explicit test that wrong-password and unknown-email 401s are byte- and timing-equivalent (this also pins H1).

## Medium

### M1 — `@Version` on an immutable Kotlin `val` (JPA optimistic-locking hazard)
**File:** `User.kt:32-33` — `@Version val version: Long = 0`. Hibernate mutates the version on every update; a Kotlin `val` (final field) makes that fragile. No `User` update occurs yet (only inserts), so it is latent, but it will surface on the first update (AC-011 password change, AC-014 timezone change).
**Fix:** `@Version var version: Long = 0` (keep other fields `val`).

### M2 — Business conflict routed via `IllegalArgumentException` + string-matched message
**Files:** `RegistrationService.kt:16-18` (`require(!existsByEmail) { "EMAIL_TAKEN" }`); `AuthExceptionHandler.kt:17-25` (`if (ex.message == "EMAIL_TAKEN")`).
Routing 409-vs-400 by string-equality on an exception message is brittle (any other `IllegalArgumentException("EMAIL_TAKEN")` would mis-map; a renamed message silently downgrades to 400). ADR-005 wants domain errors mapped to typed errors/status codes.
**Fix:** introduce a typed `DuplicateEmailException` thrown from `RegistrationService`, mapped to `409 EMAIL_TAKEN`; reserve `require` for true precondition bugs.

### M4 — `refresh_tokens.token_hash` has no unique constraint and no lookup index
**File:** `V2__users.sql:15-26`. There is an index on `user_id` but none on `token_hash`. Step 8 refresh rotation must look up a token by hash and guarantee one row per token; without a `UNIQUE` constraint a rotation bug could create duplicates silently, and without an index lookups scan the table.
**Fix:** add a forward migration `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_refresh_tokens_token_hash ON refresh_tokens (token_hash);` (ADR-003 is forward-only — do not edit `V2`), plus indexes on `rotated_from`/`expires_at` for family revocation and cleanup.

### M5 — No layer separation within the `user` module; ArchUnit only checks cycles
**Files:** all `com.productivityos.user.*` (controller, services, JPA entities, repositories in one package); `ArchitectureTest.kt:16-20` (cycle rule only).
ADR-005/system.md prescribe dependency direction API → application services → domain → persistence, and ADR-002 says module boundaries must be "enforced mechanically from the start." Currently everything coexists in one package and the only mechanical rule is "free of cycles."
**Fix:** introduce sub-packages (`user/api`, `user/app`, `user/domain`, `user/persistence`) and add ArchUnit layering rules (controllers must not call repositories directly; persistence not referenced from the API layer).

### M6 — Hardcoded dev JWT signing secret with no production guard
**File:** `application.yml:26` — `secret: ${JWT_SECRET:this-is-a-dev-secret-...}`. If `JWT_SECRET` is unset in production, the app silently starts with a publicly-known signing key, allowing token forgery. `Keys.hmacShaKeyFor` does fail-fast on a *short* key, but not on the *default* key.
**Fix:** fail startup (or require the env var) when running under a non-dev profile and the secret equals the default or is absent.

### M7 — Governance: implementation proceeds against Proposed ADRs and a Draft spec (Plan D10 unresolved)
ADR-003/004/005/006 are **Proposed** (not Accepted) and `docs/specs/users/user-management.md` is **Draft**. Plan 001 D10 states implementation "requires at least Proposed specification status and Accepted ADRs (or explicit human approval to proceed otherwise)." Writing the user-management spec closed Gap G0/D2, but D10's acceptance-bar is not formally met. This is a governance gap, not a code defect; flag for explicit human sign-off (or promote the ADRs/spec status) rather than silently treating it as accepted.

## Low

- **L1** `RefreshToken.kt:20` — `userId: UUID = UUID(0,0)` sentinel default can mask a missing assignment and persist a bogus `user_id`. Remove the default so the constructor requires `userId` (fail-fast). (Once C1-style `!!` is used the default is never hit, but it remains a footgun.)
- **L2** `RefreshToken.kt:32` — `expiresAt: Instant = Instant.now()` makes a forgotten assignment born-expired. Require `expiresAt` in the constructor (no default).
- **L3** `TokenService.kt:20` — `jwtSecret.toByteArray()` uses the platform default charset; use `Charsets.UTF_8` for portability. Also no `iss`/`aud` claims (`TokenService.kt:26-31`); `iss` is a Plan Step 7 requirement — deferred, but note it when Step 7 lands.
- **L4** `RegisterRequest.kt:9-10` — `email` has `@Email` but no `@Size(max=254)`. A >255-char value passes validation but fails the `VARCHAR(255)` column → unmapped `DataIntegrityViolationException` → 500. Add `@Size(max=254)` (RFC 5321 practical max).
- **L5** `RegisterRequest.kt:13` — `password` has `@Size(min=12)` but no maximum. Argon2id is memory-hard; an unbounded password is a DoS vector. Add a generous `@Size(max=…)` (e.g., 1024).
- **L6** `UserRepository.kt:12-14` — `existsByEmail` is a default method that calls `findByEmailIgnoreCase` and loads the entity just to null-check it. Prefer a `SELECT count`/`EXISTS` query.
- **L7** `AuthController.kt:40` — cookie `maxAge(30 * 86400)` is hardcoded while the actual refresh-token TTL comes from `app.jwt.refresh-token-ttl-days` (`TokenService.kt:18,41`). If the TTL config changes, the cookie outlives the token. Derive the cookie maxAge from the same config value.
- **L8** `AuthController.kt:22` — `Location: /api/v1/users/${user.id}` points to a `GET /api/v1/users/{id}` endpoint that does not exist yet. Acceptable as a forward reference, but the URI should resolve once the user-profile endpoint is added.
- **L9** `gradle.properties` — `org.gradle.java.installations.paths=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@21/...` is a machine-specific Homebrew path. If committed as-is it will break other machines/CI; use env-var fallback or Gradle toolchain auto-provisioning.
- **Deferred per plan (not defects):** `spring-boot-starter-security` + `SecurityConfig`/filter chain (Step 9), refresh rotation/reuse-detection/logout (Step 8), rate limiting (Step 9, AC-017), generated OpenAPI (ADR-005 §10), shared error-model/trace-id infra (Step 17). Their absence is expected at this milestone; AC-016 (no anonymous access) is moot because no domain endpoints exist yet.

## Correct

- **ADR-002 stack & layout:** Spring Boot + Kotlin modular monolith, PostgreSQL, REST under `/api/v1` — correctly realized. `HealthController` at `/api/v1/health` (public), `AuthController` at `/api/v1/auth`. Root `build.gradle.kts` declares plugin versions once; `apps/api/build.gradle.kts` holds module logic, per ADR-002.
- **ADR-003 persistence:** Flyway versioned migrations (`V1__baseline.sql`, `V2__users.sql`); UUID PKs via `gen_random_uuid()`; `timestamptz` for all timestamps; `ddl-auto: validate` (schema of record is the migration set); `open-in-view: false`; forward-only migrations; self-referential `rotated_from` FK for refresh-token family tracking; `@Version` present on `User` (intent correct, only the `val` vs `var` is wrong — M1).
- **ADR-004 auth choices:** Argon2id via `Argon2PasswordEncoder.defaultsForSpringSecurity_v5_8()` with BouncyCastle (`PasswordEncoderConfig.kt`); refresh tokens are high-entropy 32-byte random values, **hashed** (SHA-256) and persisted server-side (`LoginService.kt:54-58`); refresh token travels only via a Secure + HttpOnly + SameSite=Strict cookie scoped to `/api/v1/auth` (`AuthController.kt:35-41`); identity is never taken from a client-supplied userId — register/login derive identity only from credentials.
- **ADR-005 (partial):** `/api/v1` prefix; thin controllers that delegate to a single application service (`AuthController` → `RegistrationService`/`LoginService`); `201 Created` with `Location` for register; `200` for login; structured `{code, message}` error body for the two handled cases; `401` for auth failure; `409` for duplicate email. The structured-error *intent* is right; the *coverage* is the gap (H2/H3/H4).
- **ADR-006 (partial):** `timestamptz`/`Instant` usage; IANA timezone defaulting to UTC when omitted (`RegistrationService.kt:20-23`, AC-012 ✓); Jackson `write-dates-as-timestamps: false` for ISO-8601. The missing `Clock` bean (H5) is the gap.
- **User Management spec:** AC-001 (registration creates a login-able account ✓), AC-002 (duplicate email in any casing rejected with "email unavailable" feedback — `409 EMAIL_TAKEN` matches the spec's AC-002 wording; case-insensitive uniqueness enforced by the DB functional index `idx_users_email_lower` and `findByEmailIgnoreCase` ✓), AC-003 (`@Size(min=12)` on password ✓), AC-004 (Argon2id hash; `UserResponse` never exposes `passwordHash` ✓), AC-005 (login issues access token + refresh ✓), AC-012 (default UTC ✓), AC-013 valid-zone path ✓ (invalid-zone broken per H2). Passwords/tokens are never placed in error messages or logs.
- **Build & tests verified:** clean `compileKotlin` succeeds (C1 re-confirmed non-blocking); `gradlew test` passes — `ApplicationContextTest` (context loads + health 200 `{"status":"ok"}`) and `ArchitectureTest` (module packages free of cycles) both green against Testcontainers PostgreSQL via `@ServiceConnection`.
- **Case-insensitive email uniqueness** is enforced at the DB layer (`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_users_email_lower ON users (lower(email))`), which is the correct backstop regardless of the app-level check.
- **`AGENTS.md` addition (the only tracked diff):** the new "AI Team Workflow" section (OpenCode/Cline roles, review loop via `scripts/agent-review.sh`, human authority) is consistent with the rest of `AGENTS.md` and the `.cline`/`.opencode` tooling. No defects.
- **Tooling:** `.gitignore` correctly excludes `**/build/`, `.DS_Store`, IDE dirs; `scripts/agent-review.sh` wiring matches the documented review loop.

## Recommended changes

1. **H1** `LoginService.kt:28-33` — equalize timing with a dummy `passwordEncoder.matches(...)` against a constant hash when the user is absent; throw the same `AuthenticationException`.
2. **H2** `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` — add `@ExceptionHandler(DateTimeException::class)` → `400 {code:"INVALID_TIMEZONE"}`; optionally add a boundary IANA validator on `RegisterRequest.timezone`.
3. **H3** `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` — add `@ExceptionHandler(MethodArgumentNotValidException::class)` → `400 {code:"VALIDATION_ERROR", details:[{field,message}]}`.
4. **H4** `AuthExceptionHandler.kt` — add `@ExceptionHandler(DataIntegrityViolationException::class)` → `409 {code:"EMAIL_TAKEN"}`; rely on the DB unique index as authoritative.
5. **H5** — add a `Clock` `@Bean` (`Clock.systemUTC()`) and inject it into `TokenService` and entity-creation paths; replace all `Instant.now()` with `Instant.now(clock)`.
6. **H6** — add the Plan 001 Step 3–6 behavioral tests (registration success/duplicate/invalid, login token issuance + identical 401, repository round-trip + email uniqueness) against Testcontainers; include an explicit timing-equivalence assertion for wrong-password vs unknown-email.
7. **M1** `User.kt:32-33` — `@Version var version: Long = 0`.
8. **M2** — replace `require{}` + string-match with a typed `DuplicateEmailException` → `409`.
9. **M4** — new forward migration adding `UNIQUE INDEX … ON refresh_tokens (token_hash)` (+ indexes on `rotated_from`, `expires_at`).
10. **M5** — split `user` into `api/app/domain/persistence` sub-packages and add ArchUnit layering rules.
11. **M6** `application.yml` — guard the JWT secret so production cannot start with the default.
12. **M7** — obtain explicit human sign-off to proceed against Proposed ADRs / Draft spec, or promote their status (Plan D10).
13. **L1–L9** — remove sentinel defaults on `RefreshToken` (`userId`, `expiresAt`); use `Charsets.UTF_8` for the JWT key; add `@Size(max=254)` on `email` and a max on `password`; convert `existsByEmail` to an `EXISTS` query; derive the cookie `maxAge` from `refresh-token-ttl-days`; make `gradle.properties` JDK path portable.
14. **Forward-looking (deferred, track in later steps):** `iss` claim (Step 7), `spring-boot-starter-security` + `SecurityConfig` + rate limiting (Step 9, AC-017), refresh rotation/reuse-detection/logout (Step 8), generated OpenAPI (ADR-005 §10), shared error-model/trace-id (Step 17).
